<image-desc>This image shows the front cover of a historical book binding, likely 16th or 17th century, made of blind-stamped vellum or pigskin over boards. The surface is aged to a yellowish-tan and shows significant dark brown staining and foxing. The decoration consists of three vertical panels defined by blind-tooled lines. Within these panels are symmetrical stamped motifs: several circular medallions featuring intricate internal patterns, interspersed with stylized floral and foliage stamps, including fleur-de-lis. A metal (likely brass) catch for a clasp remains attached to the right edge; it is engraved with gothic-style lettering that appears to read "ihs" (an abbreviation for the name of Jesus). A single capital letter "A" is stamped or inscribed near the top center. At the bottom right corner, there are faint, illegible marks that may be a faded signature or shelf mark.</image-desc>

--- Page 9 ---
<lang>Latin</lang>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>

<image-desc>
A woodcut illustration of an armillary sphere, a model of objects in the sky. The sphere is enclosed in a double-line rectangular frame. At the top of the central axis is the label "Polus Arcticus" (North Pole), and at the base is "Polus Antarcticus" (South Pole). The sphere features several rings: the horizon, the meridian, and multiple celestial circles. A wide diagonal band represents the ecliptic and is engraved with the symbols of the zodiac. On the front-facing portion of the band, from left to right, are Gemini (♊), Taurus (♉), Aries (♈), Pisces (♓), Aquarius (♒), and Capricorn (♑). In the background section, Leo (♌), Virgo (♍), Libra (♎), and Scorpio (♏) are visible. At the very center of the rings sits a small globe representing the Earth. A hand, clothed in a sleeve and emerging from a stylized cloud at the bottom right, firmly grasps the vertical axis. A ribbon or scroll is entwined around the lower part of the axis, bearing the words "Sphaera Mundi" (Sphere of the World).
</image-desc>

<vocab>Polus Arcticus, Polus Antarcticus, Sphaera Mundi, armillary sphere, zodiac, Gemini, Taurus, Aries, Pisces, Aquarius, Capricorn, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio</vocab>

# ¶ Incipit cõpilatio Leupoldi ducatus Austrie filij de astroꝝ sciẽtia.

<image-desc>A large ornamental woodcut initial 'G' with white vine-work (rinceau) patterns on a dark background, enclosed in a square frame.</image-desc>
Gloriosus deus ⁊ sublimis: qui omnia verbo creauit terrā in celi medio prudenter collocauit vt corpora celestia cui virtutū suarum quas a suo creatore ⁊ ordinatore acceperāt effectus imprimerent receptaculū inuenirēt. Qui aut dñs seculoꝝ mundanis rebus quas sublimari globo posuit instabiles ⁊ caducas impressioes faciat ꝑ indiuidua superioꝛa stabilia ⁊ perpetua que a luna sursuz celi nomine designanť : solus ille ignoꝛat qui mēte obstinatus: aut carnalis vite mollicie delicatus: opera superioꝛ ⁊ passiones inferioꝛ nō cōsiderat ⁊ obseruat. Sunt quoqȝ nōnulli qui volunt a simplicibus astrologi nuncupari: vanū nomen a stultis iudicibus vsurpantes: cū suppositis philosophoꝝ positionibus motus illoꝝ depingūt ⁊ iactanter ostendūt. Sed scientiā effectuū seu fructuū non attingunt. Qui cum profecto bene circa hec aduertunt: se nō sapientes sed deuientes inueniunt. Quis enim nisi demens laboꝛaret in via: ⁊ negligeret esse in patria. Quis nisi mente captus campū araret ⁊ semina terre mādaret ⁊ suimet culpa inde fructū nō colligeret nec gustaret. Dicāt illi an motus celestiū ꝓpter eoꝝ effectus: an effectus propter motū a philosophis est quesitus. Huius questionis respōsione superposita tãq̃ certa: motuū sciām non repꝛobo sed pꝛoꝛsus necessariā appꝛobo: finē tamē bonoꝝ his que sunt ad finē iusto rōnis iudicio antepono. Ego sum inquā Leupoldus ducatus austrie fili⁹ : qui post longū ⁊ cōtinuū studiū astronomie habeo ad honoꝛē dei tenaciter in intentione omnia que de astroꝝ sciētia cõprehendi in unū volumẽ reducere. De motibus aut quia multi de his scripserunt copiose ⁊ predixerunt: sūmarie pertrãsibo : vt possim amplius ⁊ vtilius effectibus imoꝛari: de quib⁹ licet sufficienter scripserūt philosophi: nullū tamē ex eis inueni qui libroꝝ diuersoꝝ sciām in unū colligeret: ⁊ ita studētib⁹ cōpendiū generaret.

¶ Ad hec ergo meū grande ⁊ graue pꝛopositū suppliciter inuoco dei potentiā: vt in suo seruicio me cōfirmet: eiusqȝ sapiẽtiā ut meū intellectū illuminet: ⁊ suā bonitatē qua sua gratia me conseruet. Non hesitas quin deus ad ꝓficiendum in eo laboꝛe gratiā adhibeat ⁊ fauoꝛē. Quia dum homines excito ad mirabiliū operū suoꝝ cōsiderationē: ipsos cōsequenť in eius amoꝛē ⁊ in laudes pꝛuoco. Et vt nullus ambigat me p̃sens opus de sua virtute nō de mea pꝛobitate consilium reuerenter nō temerarie assumpsisse: ecce ipsū in lucē pꝛofero in die natiuitatis ipsius: in cui⁹ nomine cōceptus extitit ⁊ inceptus.

<vocab>Leupoldus de Austria, Compilatio de astrorum scientia, astronomy, astrology, medieval science, Erhard Ratdolt, creation, celestial bodies, philosophy</vocab>

¶ Titulus operis erit iste. Incipit compilatio leupoldi ducatus austrie filij de astroz scientia. Nomen autoris non querat̃. Non enĩ unus autoꝛ sed plurimi extiterũt autoꝛes. Ego enĩ fidelis illoꝛ fui obseruatoꝛ ꝛ diligẽs cõpilatoꝛ. Sapientie nrõ operi subicit̃ quicqd enim in ipso dicit̃ circa theoricã versat̃: practica võ istius sciẽtie in preceptis canonũ explicat̃. ¶ Diuiditur aũt in decẽ tractatus. ¶ Primus est de sꝑeris ꝛ earum circulis ꝛ motibus. ¶ Secũdus est de sꝑeris planetarũ ꝛ eoꝛ circulis ꝛ motibus. ¶ Tertius de probatione scientie impressionũ ꝛ quid sit hec sciẽtia. ¶ Quartus de introductorijs iudicioꝛ. ¶ Quintus de annoꝛum reuolutionibus. ¶ Sextus de aeris mutationibus. ¶ Septimus de natiuitatib⁹. ¶ Octauus de questionibus. ¶ Nonus de electionib⁹ cui annectã naturalẽ imaginũ scientiã astronomie filiã: cõtẽpnens ꝛ reprobãs illã nigromãticã diabolicã ꝛ pꝛophanã. ¶ Decimus de intẽtionib⁹ quarũ sciẽtia est admirabilis ꝛ stupenda.

<image-desc>A large decorative drop-cap "P" featuring ornate floral and leaf-like scrollwork inside a rectangular frame.</image-desc>

**P**remissa igit̃ intentione ac tractatuũ oꝛdine ab his ad quos cõpilatio pꝛesẽs peruenerit tria peto. ¶ Primũ est vt capita omniũ ꝑpositionũ locent in pꝛincipijs linearũ: quia sic eas in numeri millenariũ extendere intendo: cõmodi⁹ numerent̃ ꝛ vt tanto pꝛompti⁹ inueniant̃: ꝛ facilius cõpꝛehendant̃ quãto distinctius collocant̃. ¶ Secũdũ est vt si aliquis sꝑfluũ vel diminutũ vel inoꝛdinatũ inuenerit mihi indulgeat. Nam in tanto pelago quelibet posset submergere multitudo. ¶ Tertiũ est vt cõsiderato meo laboꝛe suaqꝝ vtilitate multas grates faciat me habere. Pôstremo qꝛ in hoc negocio est necessarius executoꝛ qui copiã eius faciat scientie dilectoꝛibus non detractoꝛib⁹ ad hec omnibus alijs pꝛeelegi Raymundũ de lauduno capitaneũ aurelianũ: nō quia fulget scdm̃ genus seculi alti sanguinis dignitate. Sed quia nitet moꝛum laudabili honestate philosophie amatoꝛ ꝛ sedulus perscrutatoꝛ: volens ꝛ in pꝛemiũ laboꝛis sui ei concedẽs vt nō minoꝛis executio censeat̃ meriti qꝝ collectoꝛi ¶ Astrologia est sciẽtia que scrutat̃ motus celestiũ coꝛpoꝛ ꝛ demōstrat. ¶ Astronomia est scientia que celestiũ coꝛpoꝛ obseruat effectus opera ꝛ mutatiōes in istis inferioꝛib⁹: ꝛ quia rerum effectus sequuntur ad illoꝛ motus ꝛ situs: necesse habuit astronomus recipere ab astrologo doctrinã: qua motus ꝛ situs celestiũ cognôsceret respectu terre in qua fuerit. ꝛ exinde iudicium de effectibus circũdaret.

<image-desc>A circular diagram depicting the geocentric model of the universe. At the center is Earth, represented as a circle divided by lines suggesting land and water. Surrounding Earth are ten concentric rings representing the celestial spheres. A vertical line of symbols in the upper half indicates the order of the planets and luminaries: Moon (☽), Mercury (☿), Venus (♀), Sun (☉), Mars (♂), Jupiter (♃), and Saturn (♄). The ring beyond these is the firmament, filled with star symbols (*). The outermost ring is divided into twelve segments containing the symbols for the signs of the Zodiac (Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius, Pisces).</image-desc>

<image-desc>A circular diagram of the celestial sphere or armillary sphere, showing the principal astronomical circles. At the top is the 'circul⁹ artic⁹' (Arctic circle). Below it is the 'Circulus cancri' (Tropic of Cancer). The central horizontal line is the 'Equinoctialis' (Equator). Below that is the 'Circulus capriconi' (Tropic of Capricorn) and at the bottom is the 'Circulus antarticus' (Antarctic circle). A diagonal band labeled 'obliqu⁹' represents the ecliptic, containing various zodiac symbols (Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo on the left; Pisces, Aquarius, Capricorn, Sagittarius, Scorpio, Libra on the right). Another diagonal line is labeled 'horizon', and a point at the top left is labeled 'Zenith'.</image-desc>

Diuersitas visionis stellarum est triplex aut quia sūt maioꝛes aut minoꝛes Nam maioꝛes citius videntur . <image-desc>
A large astronomical diagram illustrating the geometry of solar and lunar eclipses. 

In the center is a dark, solid circle labeled "Terra" (Earth). 

To the left, a diagram for "Eclypsis solis" (Solar Eclipse) shows a large circle labeled "Sol" (Sun) and a smaller circle labeled "Luna" (Moon) positioned between the Sun and the Earth. Lines radiate from the Sun, converging at the Moon and Earth to show the cone of the shadow.

To the right, a diagram for "Eclypsis lune" (Lunar Eclipse) shows a large circle labeled "Sol" (Sun) on the far right and a smaller circle labeled "Luna" (Moon) on the inner side of the Earth's shadow path. The Earth is positioned between the Sun and the Moon, casting a shadow that obscures the Moon.

The orbits and intersecting lines are enclosed within a large circular boundary representing the celestial sphere or the path of the zodiac.
</image-desc>

Along this vertical axis, three points are marked and labeled: "c Aequantis" (center of the equant), "c deferentis" (center of the deferent), and "c mundi" (center of the world/earth). <image-desc>A geometric diagram illustrating the Ptolemaic astronomical system for planetary motion. It features a large main circle (the deferent) with a vertical diameter line. Along this vertical line, three points are marked as centers: "C equantis" (the center of the equant) at the top, "C deferentis" (the center of the deferent) in the middle, and "C mundi" (the center of the world or Earth) at the bottom. Four smaller circles (epicycles) are positioned at the top, bottom, and horizontal extremes of the deferent. Several lines radiate from the centers, intersecting the epicycles and the deferent to define various angles and points of motion, such as the mean and true motions of a planet.</image-desc>

A thick outer band is colored reddish-orange, followed by a thinner white band, then a thick dark-grey/black band, and several inner white and black rings. At the top of one of the white orbits, there is a small circle representing an epicycle with a planetary body inside it. Lines originate from the center of the world to various points in the orbits. The central core of the diagram contains labels: "c def" (centrum deferentis), "equi" / "equat" (centrum equantis), and "mundi" (centrum mundi), indicating the different centers of motion used in Ptolemaic astronomy.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
An astronomical diagram illustrating the celestial mechanics of the planet Mercury. It features a large main circle (the deferent) with three smaller circles (epicycles) attached at the top, bottom, and left positions. A central point is labeled ".c mundi" (center of the world). Straight lines radiate from this center point toward the epicycles, particularly the one on the left, which contains two shaded circular points representing the planet's position. This geometric layout demonstrates the complex interaction of the deferent, epicycle, and equant points discussed in the preceding text to explain Mercury's irregular apparent motion and retrogradation.

<image-desc>A woodcut illustration depicting the planetary deity Venus. Venus is shown seated in a four-wheeled chariot, holding an arrow or spear in her right hand and wearing a winged helmet. Redditus virtutis est quando iungitur planeta alteri combusto vel qui est sub radijs ☉ vel retrogrado quia tunc non potest retinere et reddit ei quod iungitur. Et si ille sit in angulo vel succedenti erit conspicuo. Et si ille qui iungitur est cadens et cui iungitur in angulo erit finis conspicuo et si e contrario tunc illi erit initium quia applicans est levis et est in angulo sed non finis quia receptor est cadens. Et si ambo cadentes cum detrimento. Cuius exemplum est ut sit ascendens ♋ et ☽ in ♐ in .6. scilicet cadens et applicat ♂ i. 12 gra. ♊ existere: ab ascendente cadenti et tunc significat destructionem interrogationis huius et finis. Refrenatio est cum planeta volens alij iungi prius fit retrogradus pro quo destruitur ei ♂. Contrarietas accidentis fit cum aliquis planeta levis fuerit multorum graduum ut ☿ .29. in ♍ et alter ponderosior unus ut ♄ in .28. in eodem signo et tertius leuior secundo sit adhuc minus ut ♃ sit in :24. gra. eiusdem signi et antequam ♃ iungatur ♄ ☿ retrogradus fit et iungitur ♄ inde ♃ unde coniunctio ♃ et ♄ annihilatur.

Cometa est vapor terreus habens partes grossas fortiter cōiacentes ascendētēs ad superiorem partem estus: regnoꝝ ꝛ aliarū magnarū rerum alterationes significans in hoc mūdo quos alij dicunt fieri a coniunctione duorū planetarum alij ex adherentia vaporis cum lumine planete vel stelle. Johannes damascenus dicit cometā a deo fieri ad signanduz mortes regum ꝛ dissolui per deum. ¶ Comete sunt .9. ¶ Primus est saturni ꝛ est niger vel ceruleus: ꝛ cum apparet significat mortalitatem ꝛ famem ¶ Duo sequentes sunt iouis argentū ꝛ rosa. Argentū habet radiū pulcherrimū ad modum argenti purissimi q non potest aspici. Et cum apparet significat annos fertiles: ꝛ magis si iupiter tunc fuerit in signo aquatico. Rosa est magna ꝛ munda ꝛ habet faciem ad moduz hominis ꝛ color eius est vt argenti in auro cōmixti: ꝛ apparens significat mortem regum ꝛ diuituz ꝛ mutabūt res mundi ꝛ venient meliores. ¶ Martis sunt .4. feru partita tenacula ꝛ matura rubea: ꝛ omnes significāt p̄lia ꝛ terrores in mūdo: ꝛ eorum colore cognosciť malū quod significāt ꝛ ex natura signi in quo priꝯ apparent: ꝛ si apparent in oriente tunc cito eueniet quod significāt: in occidēte tardabiť. ¶ Ueneris est vnus ꝛ vocať miles ꝛ est magnus ad modum lune: ꝛ crines ꝛ radios infundet retro ꝛ percurret .12. signa ꝛ nocet regibus ꝛ potentibus: ꝛ insurgunt homines in mundo qui volunt mutare leges antiquas ꝛ adducere nouas: ꝛ peior significatio est ex parte caude. ¶ Mercurij est vnus ꝛ vocať dñs astozie ꝛ est ceruleus ꝛ paruus ꝛ cauda que est longa ꝛ cum apparet significat mortem ꝛ guerras. ¶ Feru est aspectu horribilis ꝛ vadit prope solem. ¶ Partica grofsum corpus habet ꝛ significat specialiter paucitatē aquarum ꝛ magis coniuncta veneri. Si mercurio moriētur iuuenes ꝛ sapientes. Si lune morietur populus. Si saturno mortalitas grauis erit. Si marti erunt guerre ꝛ mortalitas per gladiū. Si ioui quicqd significat in regibus ꝛ diuitibus apparebit. ¶ Cometa si apparuerit in ascendente natiuitatis vel fundationū alicuius loci vel alterius radicis vel in signo profectionis vel in ascendente reuolutionis vel in gradu directionis significat mortem nati vel destructionē illius rei cuius ascendens fuit illud signū: ꝛ si apparuerit in medij celi signo cum quis eleuatus est ad dignitatem eleuatio dignitatis periculuz imminebit intellige in signo quod fuit in medio celi in tempore eleuationis sue.

### Tractatus sextus de mutatione aeris.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap 'D' with foliate scrollwork inside a rectangular border.</image-desc>

De mutatione aeris prescire intendens: multa philosophoruz volumina perquisiui: ex quib' ppendi qd oĩs mutatio aeris fit aut ex natura signorum aut stellarũ que in eis sunt: aut ex natura planetarũ in se & in signis & eoz cõiũctionib' & applicationibus. Ideoq3 primo collegi naturã signorum & stellarũ que i ipis sunt. Secũdo naturã planetarũ in se & in signis & eoz coniunctiones & applicationes. Tercio regulas quas ponũt philosophi de mutatione aeris psciẽdas. ¶ Aries vniuersaliť tonat & choruscat: & eius picipiũ pluuias facit & ventos: medietas est tempata: extrema cõburunt & mortalitatẽ generant: & septẽtriones calefaciũt & destruũt: meridiane cõgelãt & infrigidant. ¶ Thaurus vtrãq3 cõplexionẽ tenet vlr: plus tamẽ ad calorẽ declinat: & stelle circa principiũ eius: & maxime que circa pliades sunt terremotus & vẽtos & nebulas faciũt: in medio humectãt & infrigidãt. In extremo tũc circa aldeborã ignes coruscationes & tonitrua faciũt: septẽtrionales tẽperate sunt: meridiane instabiles inordinatos mot' faciunt. ¶ Gemini vniuersaliť faciũt tẽperie in principio humectãt & destruunt: in medio tẽporãt: i fine cõmiscẽt: septẽtrionales vẽtos & terremot' faciũt: meridiane ignee sũt & cõburũt. ¶ Cãcer generaliť facit serenitatẽ & calorẽ: & in pricipio circa psepe calidũ & turbidũ facit aerẽ & terremot': q sunt in medio tẽporãt: in extremo vẽtos faciũt: septẽtrionales & meridiane cõbuste sunt. ¶ Leo vniuersaliter calidũ & turbidũ facit aerẽ & silr ei' principiũ & mortalitatẽ: mediũ tẽporat: extremũ humectat & destruit: septẽtrionales ignee sũt & cõburũt: meridiane humectãt. ¶ Virgo vniuersaliť humectat & tonat: principiũ calefacit & destruit: mediũ tempat et extremũ. ¶ Libra vniuersaliť variat primũ: & mediũ tempat: extremũ est aquaticũ: et septentrionales ventos: meridiane humidũ et mortalitatẽ faciunt. ¶ Scorpio vniuersaliť tonat et est igneum et in principio ventos nimiũ facit: et in medio temporat: et in fine sue stelle terremotum faciunt: septẽtrionales comburunt: meridiane humectant.

--- Page 122 ---
<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>text</page-type>

<image-desc>
A square astrological chart (horoscope) common in early modern European manuscripts and prints. It consists of a large square divided into twelve triangular houses. 
- In the central square, text reads: "Figura ista natũ diuici as sig̃t copiosas acqrere" (This figure signifies a nativity acquiring copious riches).
- The houses contain various zodiac signs and planetary symbols.

# Venus

<image-desc>
A woodcut illustration depicting the goddess Venus on a celestial chariot moving toward the left. The chariot is pulled by two eagles. Venus is seated on the right, wearing a gown and a winged helmet, holding an arrow or spear. In front of her stands a small, winged Cupid, blindfolded and holding a bow. The chariot's wheels contain the zodiac signs of Taurus (left) and Libra (right), which are the houses of Venus. A five-pointed star is emblazoned on the side of the chariot. ## ¶Tractatus decimus de intentionibus.

<image-desc>Large ornamental drop-cap initial 'I' decorated with intricate vine and foliate motifs in a rectangular frame.</image-desc>Intentionum volentis tibi facere questionez cum ad hoc ad te venerit: interposito sibi silencio: scias per dominuz ascendentis et locum eius: ordinata figura celi et positis planetz in locis suis in hora qua ille querere intendebat Si enim dominus ascendentis est in prima domo volebat querere de seipso: si in secunda de substantia: z sic per omnes domos prout significant.
